Frontmatter override: posts/wiki gain an optional level: 'beginner' | 'intermediate' | 'advanced' field so authors can short-circuit the auto-score when a post is more or less complex than the metric predicts (e.g. a philosophy-heavy essay with few wikilinks that should still be labeled advanced). Analyzer is pure and body-string-only, no DOM / graph needed, so it's safe to call at getStaticPaths time without caching. Part of E-23 Reading Complexity (Wave 4). 2026-06-29T20:02:40Z	bafd767720924d31a06c262bc62198b08435201d	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/bafd767720924d31a06c262bc62198b08435201d	ci: ensure NODE_OPTIONS reaches astro + split pagefind into its own process Build still OOMed at 4128 MB heap in spite of NODE_OPTIONS=7168 in the workflow env. Two root causes: 1. bun run build did not forward NODE_OPTIONS to astro GitHub Actions ubuntu-latest provides 7+ GiB of RAM, but the v8 heap stayed pinned at the default 4 GiB. Stack traces show v8 GC, so astro IS running on node, but bun's exec path apparently strips or ignores the parent process's NODE_OPTIONS for the spawned binary (see oven-sh/bun#6548; multiple reports of the same symptom). Three-layer defense so any of them is sufficient: - package.json: 'build' script now starts with NODE_OPTIONS='--max-old-space-size=7168' astro build This sets the env directly on astro's process, not on bun's, bypassing any inheritance issue. - workflow step: inline before bun run, in addition to the existing job-level env:. - workflow env: NODE_OPTIONS remains as the final safety net. 2. astro-pagefind ran in-process via astro:build:done The integration calls createIndex(), addDirectory(dist), and writeFiles() all inside the same Node process that just produced 1500 HTML pages. Pagefind has to load every HTML into memory to build the inverted index, which by itself can be ~500 MB-1 GB on our content set. Combined with cached content-graph/related/taxonomy Maps left over from the page render phase, the in-process spike is enough to break the 4 GiB ceiling even before our NODE_OPTIONS fix. Mitigation: - astro.config.mjs: remove pagefind() from integrations[] (and the import). The build now ends right after astro's regular post-build hooks; the cached collections are released when the node process exits. - package.json: 'build:pagefind' = 'pagefind --site dist' invokes the Rust pagefind CLI directly. Rust runtime, separate process, v8 heap untouched. Typical RSS for this CLI is well under 200 MB even on 1500 pages. - workflows: new 'Build Pagefind index' step runs after the build step in both deploy.yml and pr-checks.yml. Net effect: the build uses up to 7 GiB if it needs to, pagefind starts fresh. Side effects: - Dev server no longer ships /pagefind/pagefind.js automatically. The in-page search modal (SearchModal, /search page, 404 suggester) all gracefully fall back to 'index not loaded' in dev mode. They worked the same way before, since astro-pagefind only emits the index on build anyway. - Production parity is preserved: dist/pagefind/ is still produced, GitHub Pages still serves it, search still hits the same chunks. 2026-06-29T10:04:36Z	11ecbb64bf419efe980753a7e7b987b66f30d690	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/11ecbb64bf419efe980753a7e7b987b66f30d690	fix(mdx): close first variant in knuth-x + ZWSP for blank-line template literals Two unrelated fixes, both rooted in the same build failure. 1. knuth-x.mdx: missing object literal terminator (the real bug) The build kept failing on knuth-x.mdx:129:5 with acorn-jsx's 'parsePropertyName' error. Background diff against working files (dp.mdx, code-with-output-showcase.mdx) revealed the variant object for the C++ snippet was not closed before the Python variant began: }`, { <- missing }, language: "python", ... That is plain JS syntax error inside the variants={[...]} array, not an mdx-js limitation - the parser sees `,{ where it expects `,}, {. Inserted the missing ` },` between the two variants. Equivalent typos were checked across the whole src/content tree and only this one file is affected. 2. vite-mdx-body-escape: replace bare blank lines inside JSX prop template literals with a zero-width space. Originally proposed as a fix for the knuth-x error and turned out to be unrelated, but it is still worth keeping. mdx-js issue #1945 and #195 document the long-standing limitation that an empty line inside a multi-line template literal passed to a JSX attribute terminates the surrounding markdown paragraph and breaks the compiler. The plugin now detects empty lines that opened inside a template literal and still close inside one, and emits U+200B (ZERO WIDTH SPACE) instead. The character is invisible in browsers, does not change the rendered code snippet, and survives the markdown paragraph detection because the line is no longer 'empty'. This protects future wiki entries whose author keeps blank lines in their pasted snippets - the same shape that the official mdx-js troubleshooting docs call out as still broken. Verified: knuth-x.mdx now has a balanced variants array. astro check 0 errors. Plugin standalone regression still produces the expected escape on the hazard tests.

2026-06-29T06:16:15Z	a5f44dab31e78a2e53fd115537dc31a803ac31e5	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/a5f44dab31e78a2e53fd115537dc31a803ac31e5	feat(mdx): expand body-escape plugin with multi-line template literal tracking Followup to the earlier integration. Build was reaching the plugin but not escaping certain bodies because the state machine did not track JSX multi-line template literals correctly. Plugin changes (src/plugins/vite-mdx-body-escape.mjs): - updateJsxDepth now toggles inTemplateLiteral on every unescaped backtick and skips angle-bracket counting while inside one. Previously every `<` inside a <CodeWithOutput code={`...`}/> template literal was counted as a JSX tag opening, so jsxDepth kept growing past the real `/>` closer and the rest of the document stayed flagged as 'inside JSX' (escape disabled). Tracking template literal state via a module-scope flag lets depth balance restore correctly on the component's actual close. - New escapeTablePipesInBackticks: in markdown table rows, `...|...` inside inline backticks gets the pipe escaped (`\|`) before the markdown table parser runs. This protects expressions like `mask | (1 << i)` whose backticks would otherwise be split by the table cell delimiter, leaving the bit-shift exposed. - Curly-brace hazard set expanded to include unprotected `|`, `,`, `;` characters inside body `{...}` (set-builder, surreal numbers). Previously only letters and math operators triggered escape. - Uppercase `<X>` with a 1-2 character tag name is now escaped as `\<`. JSX components on the site are all 3+ letters (<CodeWithOutput>, <ChartJs>, <UrlPreview>, ...), so single-letter `<V>`, `<T>` etc. are nearly always C++ template parameters or generic notation in prose. - Lowercase `<i)`-style tag attempts now check the boundary after the tag name. `<i>`, `<i class=...>`, `<i />` survive (real HTML); `<i)`, `<i,`, `<i=` get escaped. Build verification: before this commit, dp.mdx:194 `(1<<i)` and the related bitmask-table lines crashed the parser. After: confirmed via isolated plugin run that the offending lines are rewritten to `(1\<\<i)`. Build now advances much further and exposes only mdx-js limitations the plugin cannot fix (e.g. long multi-line cpp inside <CodeWithOutput code={`...`}/> where mdx-js parses the template literal contents as JSX during its markdown phase). Those cases need an author-side fix: switch from inline template literals to fenced ```cpp blocks beside the component. Mermaid changes (from same task, included for atomicity of build setup): - src/features/mermaid/lib/mermaid-render.ts: pin CDN to mermaid@11.6.0 (was floating @11). Per-node try/catch around mermaid.run so a single broken diagram does not blank the whole page; failures now render an inline error card with the parser message and the original source. - src/features/mermaid/styles/mermaid.css: .mermaid-error styles using color-mix on --color-danger. astro check: 0 errors. lint:no-em-dash: 1625 files clean. Plugin verified with isolated regression tests for: inline hazard, HTML tag, fenced code block, JSX block, comparison operator.

Also added scripts/fix-mdx-curly-braces.mjs that finds the same class of hazard across the wiki tree. Reusing the indent / JSX-line / fenced- code guards from fix-mdx-jsx-arrows.mjs, it surfaces ~383 candidate spots across 123 files. NOT applied automatically: dry-run review shows the scanner cannot tell apart JSX prop multi-line template literals (safe) from prose math notation (hazard) without parsing MDX properly. Operator should review the dry-run output and apply selectively, e.g. 'bun scripts/fix-mdx-curly-braces.mjs --files <list>'. Further build attempts will keep revealing one hazard per wiki entry until the user-authored content is swept. The two patterns that cause hard build stops: body `<=`, `<-`, `<->` -> handled by fix-mdx-jsx-arrows.mjs body `{...}` with letters -> handled by fix-mdx-curly-braces.mjs astro check: 0 errors.
2026-06-25T07:45:14Z	374d3809ac515dadad1c93e67f0fa2e327a5f45e	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/374d3809ac515dadad1c93e67f0fa2e327a5f45e	fix(mdx): replace ASCII <= / <- / <-> in body text with unicode (Phase MDX-fix) Followup to dp-connection-profile.mdx:21:78 build failure 'Unexpected character `=` (U+003D) before name'. Root cause: MDX treats `<` in body text as the start of a JSX tag, so `H <= 10` becomes an opening tag `<=` and the `=` cannot be a tag name. Added scripts/fix-mdx-jsx-arrows.mjs to scan wiki body text and substitute only the unambiguously hazardous bigraphs: <- -> ← (only with whitespace on both sides) <= -> ≤ (only followed by space/tab/EOL/closing paren) <-> -> ↔ The scanner: - skips lines inside fenced code blocks (``` toggle) - skips lines that look like JSX (start with `<`, `import`, `export`) - skips lines with 4+ leading spaces or a leading tab (indented code blocks and JSX-child template-literal bodies) - masks inline backtick spans before matching `>=` and `->` are NOT touched even though they are common in body text: `>` does not start a JSX tag, so they are not hazards (only readability differences). Leaving them alone keeps this commit narrow and reversible. 19 files / 50 substitutions applied. The user-reported dp-connection- profile.mdx hazard is included. Followup build hit a different class of hazard: `{0, ..., N-1}` in dp-bitfield.mdx:26 trips acorn-jsx as a spread expression. That is a LaTeX-style set literal in body text and needs a separate pass (wrap in inline code). Same class as the earlier fft-ntt.mdx fix.

2026-06-20T08:24:47Z	e45a9ea50fb6fb7391a29d8448ae0a2727d206a3	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/e45a9ea50fb6fb7391a29d8448ae0a2727d206a3	feat(wiki-filter): paginate filtered results (WIKI_PER_PAGE per page) The wiki search/tag filter used to render every match into a single flat <ul> via innerHTML, so a query like 'java' dumped 87 items into the page with no pagination. The default wiki list paginates 20 per page (WIKI_PER_PAGE) via the Astro server route, so the filtered view visibly diverged once results crossed that threshold. Adds a client-side pagination layer to the filter: - URL state: `?search=foo&tag=bar&page=N`, omitted when N=1; clamped on read and on render so out-of-range `?page=999` lands on lastPage instead of an empty list. - Page state resets to 1 whenever the query string or active tag changes (typing, Escape-to-clear, tag chip toggle). - New <nav data-wiki-filtered-pagination> container in /wiki/[...page].astro. Filled with HTML that mirrors shared/ui/Pagination.astro's ellipsis algorithm and Tailwind classes, so the filter pagination looks identical to the default list pagination. Buttons (not anchors) because the change is client-side state only. - Count text grows to '검색 결과 N개 중 a–b · p / total · 전체 …' when paginated, stays compact otherwise. - Event delegation on the persistent container handles clicks across re-renders without rebinding per button. - Page changes smooth-scroll the filtered section back into view. Verified via Playwright: 87-result query renders 20 items on page 1 with buttons [← 이전 (disabled), 1*, 2, 3, 4, 5, 다음 →]; clicking 3 shows items 41-60 and syncs ?page=3; typing a new query that returns 14 results hides the pagination and drops ?page= from the URL; direct nav to ?page=999 clamps to page 5.
2026-06-20T08:24:29Z	29ce6cf90f1fbc067a1d9a4973a90f570799c118	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/29ce6cf90f1fbc067a1d9a4973a90f570799c118	fix(search): show clear fallback when Pagefind index is unavailable The Pagefind index is generated by astro-pagefind at build time only, so `/pagefind/pagefind.js` always 404s in `bun dev`. The previous doSearch() treated a failed Pagefind load the same way as a stale token race ('return silently'), leaving stats stuck at '검색 중…' and the results region empty forever. The user-visible effect: type into the search modal in dev → nothing happens, no error, no hint. loadPagefind() now records the failure once, so subsequent keystrokes short-circuit instead of repeatedly attempting (and re-erroring on) the dynamic import. doSearch() distinguishes a missing module from a stale token and renders an explicit fallback message: - dev: explains the Pagefind+build constraint, points to `bun run build && bun preview` for real testing, and notes that command (>), tag (#), and help (?) modes still work. - prod: short 'try again later' message (covers transient CDN / CSP / network failures). Verified via Playwright in dev: typing 'java' now renders the dev fallback message; switching to '> wiki' still produces the command result ('위키') with the command-mode badge.
2026-06-19T15:56:30Z	fdf11b2c8ce97e6759b887eaf455c08e344540c2	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/fdf11b2c8ce97e6759b887eaf455c08e344540c2	fix(wiki-list): style wiki-list classes globally so filter results match The wiki filter (src/features/wiki-filter) renders search/tag results by injecting <li><a class="wiki-list-item">…</a></li> into a separate <ul data-wiki-filtered-list> via innerHTML. Astro scopes <style> blocks by appending [data-astro-cid-X] to every selector and every server- rendered element. innerHTML-injected nodes never get a cid attribute, so neither WikiList.astro's nor the [...page].astro duplicate <style> block (added previously to compensate, with the same scoping bug) could reach those nodes. The filter results rendered as a flat, padding-less, border-less list. Marking WikiList.astro's <style> with is:global drops the cid attribute selectors so the rules apply to every .wiki-list / .wiki-list-item on the page, server-rendered or injected. The page-level duplicate is no longer needed, so it's removed. Verified via Playwright: default-list and filtered-list <li> + <a> now have identical computed borderBottomWidth (1px), display (block), and padding (9.6px).

Useful runs: bun scripts/audit-alt-text.mjs # summary + files needing attention bun scripts/audit-alt-text.mjs --verbose # include line/src per finding bun scripts/audit-alt-text.mjs --json # machine-readable output bun scripts/audit-alt-text.mjs --strict # exit 1 if any attention items Baseline at commit time: 64 image refs, 16 need attention (14 content images in my-life/ and BOJ posts, 2 thumbnail listing templates in src/pages/posts/[...page].astro and category/[category]/[...page].astro).

Category (+0.15) and series (+0.10) bonuses are preserved. RelatedEntry gains an optional 'signals' field carrying the per-signal contribution for future diagnostic UI; existing 'shared' (tag display) stays unchanged so the RelatedPosts.astro component does not need to change. The ComputeRelatedOptions signature is also unchanged. The index loader is cached once per build and reuses getContentGraph() to avoid re-scanning markdown bodies.

2026-06-05T12:32:10Z	432f6fdeb14f2182de2b65c7007b233dacbd7243	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/432f6fdeb14f2182de2b65c7007b233dacbd7243	fix(studio): hide history + palette dialogs by default User report: even after the previous close-button fix, the 📜 작업 이력 modal kept floating in the studio editor screen — 'this should only appear when I want it'. Root cause: the CSS for .studio-history-dialog (and same shape for .studio-palette-dialog) set 'display: flex' unconditionally: .studio-history-dialog { ... display: flex; flex-direction: column; overflow: hidden; } The browser default for <dialog> is 'display: none' until the open attribute is set (or showModal() is called). Our rule overrode that with the same specificity but later in cascade, so the element was laid out as a 480×99 / 520×90 box from the moment the page loaded, even with no open attribute. The dialog was never 'open', just 'always rendered'. Clicking ✕ or pressing Esc had no effect because there was no modal state to close — the visible thing was just the laid-out element. The previous fix (generic close handler + label preservation) was correct but addressed the wrong layer. Followed the existing .studio-dialog pattern (the only one that already worked): .studio-dialog[open] { display: flex; flex-direction: column; } .studio-dialog:not([open]) { display: none !important; } Applied the same shape to .studio-palette-dialog and .studio-history-dialog. 'overflow: hidden' stays in the base rule because it doesn't affect visibility. Verified live on /_studio/ after fresh page load — all 10 dialogs on the page (library, icon, help, new, palette, history, search-modal, mermaid-zoom, image-lightbox, asset) now report display='none' and takesLayoutSpace=false until explicitly opened: - Cmd+Shift+H → history dialog appears (display:flex, open=true) - ✕ click → display:none, open=false, gone from layout - Cmd+K → palette dialog appears - close() → display:none, palette gone bun astro check clean (198 files, 0/0/91). bun run lint clean.
2026-06-05T12:23:05Z	6036f5aa2facd285102374784064afe99fb4ac1d	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/6036f5aa2facd285102374784064afe99fb4ac1d	fix(studio): history / help dialogs were impossible to close, and undo/redo overwrote panel labels Two issues found while reviewing the history feature after a user report that '작업이력이 계속 떠있는' (the history panel kept staying open). # 1. Close button only worked for library + new dialogs The document-level click handler hard-coded both dialogs: if ((e.target as HTMLElement).closest('[data-studio-dialog-close]')) { ui.libraryDialog.close(); ui.newDialog.close(); ui.newDialog.removeAttribute('data-mode'); } But four dialogs in studio.astro share '[data-studio-dialog-close]' on their ✕ button: - #studio-library-dialog - #studio-help-dialog ← also silently broken - #studio-new-dialog - #studio-history-dialog ← the reported case Clicking ✕ on the help or history dialog ran the handler, called .close() on the wrong two dialogs (already closed, so no-op), and left the actually-open dialog floating. The user could only escape via the Esc key. Made the handler generic — find the nearest <dialog> ancestor of the clicked element and close that one. The 'data-mode' side effect is preserved only when the closed dialog is ui.newDialog (the only one that uses that attribute). # 2. undo/redo overwrote the panel labels with '(redo)' / '(undo)' The previous implementation: export function undo(): void { if (past.length === 0) return; const cur = snapshotJson(); if (cur !== null) future.push({ snap: cur, label: '(redo)', kind: 'other', timestamp: Date.now() }); const prev = past.pop()!; ... } After undoing any meaningful mutation (e.g. '메타 수정: title'), the history panel showed: · (redo) 방금 ← future — 현재 위치 — ✎ 메타 수정: title 방금 ← past The future row carried no kind icon and no description of what the user would re-apply by clicking. After a few undo/redo cycles the panel was filled with '(undo)' / '(redo)' entries. The label of past[N] is by construction the label of the mutation that was applied to past[N].snap to produce the current state. So: - On undo, the entry we push onto 'future' represents that same mutation (which is what the user would redo). We now copy label/kind/timestamp from past[-1]. - On redo, the entry we push onto 'past' represents the just-redone mutation, whose label/kind we copy from future[-1]. Preserving the original timestamp keeps the '5분 전' / '1시간 전' display anchored to when the user actually did the action, not when they last toggled it through undo/redo. Verified nothing else in src/dev-only/ depends on the literal '(undo)' / '(redo)' label strings. Verified live via playwright on /_studio/: - Cmd+Shift+H opens history panel. - Clicking ✕ on history dialog now closes it (was stuck open). - Clicking ✕ on help dialog now closes it (was stuck open). - new-dialog ✕ still closes AND still clears 'data-mode'. - After typing two title changes and Cmd+Z once, the panel shows '✎ 메타 수정: title 방금' on both sides of '— 현재 위치 —', no '(redo)' / '(undo)' placeholders. - After Cmd+Shift+Z (redo), both rows correctly land in 'past' with the same meaningful label. bun astro check clean (198 files, 0/0/91). bun run lint clean.

2026-05-26T05:58:13Z	c02245b3fa0defb57b36482ca023e618a4122855	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/c02245b3fa0defb57b36482ca023e618a4122855	fix(code-with-output): actually clip overflow so long content doesn't spill Previous max-height: min(70vh, 720px) alone did not work. The grid container's max-height capped its own box, but the implicit row track defaulted to minmax(auto, auto) — and auto's min is max-content. With a tall <pre> child the row track wanted to be 1060px, every grid item stretched to that 1060px (via align-items: stretch), and the items visually overflowed past the capped container because the container's overflow was the CSS default (visible). The result: a Fibonacci dump that ran straight through into the next section's heading. Fix: - grid-template-rows: minmax(0, 1fr) — explicitly let the row shrink below max-content. With max-height bounding the container, 1fr now resolves to at most 720px instead of the row's natural size. - overflow: hidden on .cwo-panes — defensive clip; nothing should visually escape the grid even if a future child misbehaves. - min-height: 0 on .cwo-pane — flex children must be allowed to shrink below content for the inner .cwo-body's overflow: auto to start scrolling. - vertical mode (data-vertical='true') gets the same minmax(0, 1fr) treatment on its code+output rows so the same overflow can't appear when codeWidth=100. Verified across all 14 figures in the showcase post: - Short figures (sections 1, 2, 5, 11, 12) stay at their natural height (81–264px); max-height never kicks in. - Section 10 (Fibonacci 100, content 1024px tall) now caps at 694px with the inner output body scrolling — bodyScrollable: true, panes scrollHeight === clientHeight, 32px gap to section 11 below. - Multi-case tab switching still adapts the figure to the active case's natural height up to the same cap. Ultraworked with [Sisyphus](https://github.com/code-yeongyu/oh-my-openagent) Co-authored-by: Sisyphus <clio-agent@sisyphuslabs.ai>

2026-05-25T11:54:47Z	c3b9e77f99331d86e8a77a4736dfd4a95bf38f03	https://github.com/shinkeonkim/shinkeonkim.github.io/commit/c3b9e77f99331d86e8a77a4736dfd4a95bf38f03	fix(ga): re-fire page_view on view-transition swap BaseLayout uses <ClientRouter /> for SPA-style navigation, which swaps the DOM in place without reloading. The inline gtag('config', ..., { send_page_view: true }) only runs once on the initial page load, so GA never receives page_view events for subsequent in-app navigations — this is why the GA console reports 'no data collection detected' even though the tag itself is firing. Listen for 'astro:after-swap' (fires only on view-transition swaps, not the initial load) and dispatch a manual page_view event with the new URL + title. Initial load is still covered by the original 'config' call, so there is no double-count. Ultraworked with [Sisyphus](https://github.com/code-yeongyu/oh-my-openagent) Co-authored-by: Sisyphus <clio-agent@sisyphuslabs.ai>
